# Notifications in the Browser (6c-2) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Accounts with no chat get reminders, event alerts, a short day line, the partner notices and the Google reconnect message as Web Push notifications on the devices where they turn them on.

**Architecture:** One device table (`PushDevice`, read and written only in `src/services/web-push/devices.ts`), one sender (`sendWebPush`, `web-push` 3.6.7 building each request and `fetchWithRetry` sending it), one reach rule (`canReachByPush`) read by every Telegram gate, and a push branch inside each send's existing no-chat branch. A root service worker (`public/sw.js`) shows a notification and opens the app; the Settings card and the home banner turn notifications on in one tap. Everything stays hidden until `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` and `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` are set.

**Tech Stack:** Next.js 16, Prisma 5.22 + Neon, Upstash Redis, next-intl, `web-push` 3.6.7, the repo's smoke scripts (tsx) in the fake env.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-web-push-design.md` (approved by the owner 2026-11-06, with its §8 legal texts and rulings 4-9).

**Where the work happens:** a worktree at `$WT` on branch `feat/web-push` from main `d619f289`.
- `WT=/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-razielgershoni-development-famcalbot/0d239296-a217-49f9-8833-757c9d9a2df6/scratchpad/wt-push`
- `RUN="env SMOKE_ROOT=$WT bash /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-razielgershoni-development-famcalbot/0d239296-a217-49f9-8833-757c9d9a2df6/scratchpad/6c1/run-smoke.sh"`: runs one `scripts/<name>.ts` with the fake env (every `.env*` name blanked, the sign-in and VAPID names blank). A smoke that needs VAPID keys makes a pair with `webpush.generateVAPIDKeys()` and sets them itself.
- Every commit message ends with:
  ```
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_017Qxen88HvHhmznBZG7Yo31
  ```

## Global Constraints

- Only accounts with no chat (`hasChat` false) get push. Telegram and WhatsApp code paths stay byte-for-byte as they are (spec decision 1).
- No Done or Snooze buttons on a notification; a tap opens the home (`appPageUrl(lang, 'dashboard', id)`), the Google reconnect opens the page Telegram's button opens (spec decision 3, §2.2).
- Sends: reminders, event alerts, the morning and evening day line (no Gemini, no `incrementUsage`, nothing on an empty day), invite accepted, the partner's Done (silent), the Google reconnect. Nothing else.
- `PushDevice` is read and written only in `src/services/web-push/devices.ts`; at most 10 devices per account (an 11th replaces the oldest).
- The pause: reminders, alerts, briefings and the reconnect ask `isPaused` with their send's existing kind; their TTL stops before the next pause window; no push waits more than 86400 s. The partner notices aren't paused (as on Telegram).
- No console line, Sentry field, cache entry or admin view holds an endpoint, a key, a notification's title, body or payload, an email, a code, a token, a state or a name. Log the user id, the kind, counts, a status.
- Every push service, Google's FCM included, goes through `fetchWithRetry`; never a retry loop, sleep or timeout of our own; never `web-push`'s own `sendNotification`.
- An idle FamCal makes no new database query; the alert cache carries only a has-device flag.
- The four writers that move `sessionsValidAfter` (sign out on all devices, removing a way, a suspension, a ban) delete the account's devices in the same write (Remove keeps the caller's); gaining a chat deletes them.
- Subscribing needs a sign-in from the last hour (`refuseStaleSignIn`), except an endpoint the account already has; the routes refuse a Mini App request and an account with a chat.
- Copy in `messages/{en,he,ru}.json` only (`bot.push.*` server-side, `settings.push.*` and the banner keys in the app), all three languages together, no emoji, natural gender-neutral Hebrew, formal Russian with no-break spaces after one-letter words and before em dashes. Lucide icons only.
- Times shown in the person's zone, 24-hour hh:mm (`instantToWallTime` for logic; never `fromZonedTime` or local `Date` setters, CLAUDE.md's wall-time rule).
- Never run `npm run build` (it migrates prod): `npx tsc --noEmit -p .`, `npx eslint <files>`, and the controller's CI mirror. Migrations are hand-written and idempotent.
- The legal texts (§8) ship as approved, in all three languages at once, with both `LEGAL_UPDATED` dates on the ship day.

## Review Focus

1. Hebrew and Russian notifications at the byte cap: a long Hebrew title is cut with "…" on a character boundary, `dir` is `rtl` for Hebrew, and the payload stays ≤ 2048 bytes of UTF-8 (Task 2's payload checks).
2. A person outside Israel: the day line's times, the briefing hour and the pause window are in their own zone, never the server's or Israel's (Task 10's New York case, Task 2's TTL to the pause window).
3. "Remind us both" with one partner on Telegram and one on push: each gets their own channel; a Done by one edits the other's Telegram ping or pushes a silent "Done by" to the no-chat one (Tasks 8 and 11).
4. Push off in production (no VAPID keys): no card switch, no banner action, the routes answer 503 (DELETE still works), the senders log today's lines, nothing throws at import (Tasks 1, 3, 5).
5. A browser on a push service outside `PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS`: refused with 400 `bad-input`, and the log line names the endpoint's host only, so the list can be widened from the logs (Task 3, ruling R3).

## Controller rulings (apply them; they win over a task's text)

- **R1:** "Notifications are on" goes only to the device that just turned them on: `PushSendInput.onlyEndpoint` (Task 2) and the route passes the endpoint (Task 3). Applied in the task text below.
- **R2:** `signInAgain` unsubscribes the browser without a DELETE (it has no user id); the row goes at the next send's 404/410. Accepted as drafted.
- **R3:** In `POST /api/push/devices`'s bad-input branch, when `endpoint` is an https URL whose host isn't a push service, the log line is `[push] user <id>: subscribe refused: unknown push service <host>` (the host only); Task 3's smoke asserts it, and that no path or query is logged.
- **R4:** Accepted as drafted: DELETE answers while push is off; `/sw.js` has `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-store, must-revalidate`; the manifest link is on the public pages too; the app-open check runs on the home and in the Settings card; a no-chat recipient with no device is claimed before the creator's plan check (Task 8).
- **R5:** Endpoint hosts allowed: `PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS` (FCM, Apple, Mozilla, WNS) and their subdomains (Task 1), against server-side request forgery.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `SELLER.email` (`src/lib/seller.ts:27`, `'privacy@famcal.bot'`); `getUserById` (`src/services/user-service.ts:36`); `syncReminderCacheEntry` (`src/services/reminder-cache.ts:163`); `safeErrorSummary`, `SafeSignInError` (`src/services/web-signin/safe-error.ts:13,21`); `captureError` (`src/lib/error-capture.ts:21`).
- Produces (contract, exact):
  ```ts
  // src/services/web-push/config.ts
  export const PUSH_ENV = ['VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY', 'VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY'] as const;
  export function pushConfigured(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): boolean;
  export function vapidPublicKey(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string | null;
  export function vapidSubject(): string; // 'mailto:privacy@famcal.bot'
  // src/services/web-push/devices.ts
  export const MAX_DEVICES = 10;
  export interface PushKeys { p256dh: string; auth: string }
  export interface PushDeviceInput { endpoint: string; keys: PushKeys }
  export interface StoredDevice { id: string; endpoint: string; p256dh: string; auth: string }
  export function isValidDeviceInput(x: unknown): x is PushDeviceInput;
  export async function addDevice(userId: number, input: PushDeviceInput): Promise<{ moved: boolean; count: number }>;
  export async function isOwnDevice(userId: number, endpoint: string): Promise<boolean>;
  export async function deleteOwnDevice(userId: number, endpoint: string): Promise<boolean>;
  export async function deleteDevicesOf(userIds: number | number[], opts?: { keep?: string | null; tx?: Prisma.TransactionClient }): Promise<number>;
  export async function devicesForSend(userId: number): Promise<StoredDevice[]>;
  export async function deleteGoneDevice(id: string): Promise<void>;
  export async function afterDevicesChanged(userIds: number[]): Promise<void>;
  ```
- Produces (beyond the contract; later tasks may use them):
  ```ts
  // src/services/web-push/config.ts
  export function missingPushEnv(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string[];  // the unset/blank names, PUSH_ENV order (pushConfigured and the startup warning share it)
  export function vapidKeys(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): { publicKey: string; privateKey: string } | null; // send.ts (Task 2) signs with it
  export const PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS: readonly ['fcm.googleapis.com', 'push.apple.com', 'push.services.mozilla.com', 'notify.windows.com'];
  export function isPushServiceUrl(url: URL): boolean; // https, no port, no user info, host is one of PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS or under one
  // src/services/web-push/devices.ts
  export function isValidEndpoint(x: unknown): x is string; // a string of at most 1024 characters that is a push service URL (isPushServiceUrl); Task 6's Remove reads it
  // src/lib/startup-checks.ts
  export function pushSetupWarnings(env: Record<string, string | undefined>): string[]; // ['Browser notifications are off: X and Y are not set'] or []
  ```
- Note for Task 3 (routes): `addDevice`, `isOwnDevice`, `deleteOwnDevice` let a database error through. A Prisma error's message can print the query's values (here an endpoint and keys), so a route logs it only as `safeErrorSummary(error)` and reports it only through `captureSignInFailure(error, '<context>', { user_id, api_route })` (`src/services/web-signin/safe-error.ts:31`), never `captureError(error)` with the raw error.
- Note for Task 9: `afterDevicesChanged` loads the account with `getUserById` and calls `syncReminderCacheEntry(user)`. Task 9 changes the cache's admission; if it needs the device count there, it adds the count in `syncReminderCacheEntry` or here, in this one place.

**Decision taken here (flag for review):** `isValidDeviceInput` also requires the endpoint's host to be a known push service (`PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS`). The server POSTs to every stored endpoint, so without this a signed-in person could make FamCal's server POST to any https host (server-side request forgery). The four services are the ones spec §3.4 names. A browser on another push service gets the 400 bad-input answer (Task 3), which is logged.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

  Create `scripts/smoke-push-devices.ts`:

  ```ts
  // Smoke test for the browser notifications' device store (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-web-push-design.md §3.1,
  // §3.9; plan 6c-2 Task 1): src/services/web-push/config.ts and devices.ts, the PushDevice table and its migration.
  //
  // What it pins:
  //   - config: push is on only with both VAPID variables set (blank is unset), the public key read at call time, the
  //     contact claim is FamCal's own address, and the push services a device may point at;
  //   - isValidDeviceInput: an https endpoint of a known push service (no port, no user info, at most 1024 characters),
  //     p256dh 80-100 and auth 16-32 base64url characters; anything else is refused;
  //   - addDevice: one row per endpoint; the same browser again keeps one row (new keys, a new turn-on time); an endpoint
  //     another account holds moves to this one; an 11th device removes the oldest; every account touched is re-synced
  //     in the event alert cache;
  //   - isOwnDevice / deleteOwnDevice: only the caller's own row; deleteDevicesOf: one account or several, keeping the
  //     endpoint named, inside a caller's transaction without a re-sync (the caller does it after the commit), and no
  //     query for no account; devicesForSend: the four fields; deleteGoneDevice: deletes and re-syncs, never throws;
  //   - afterDevicesChanged never throws: a failure is logged and reported by its kind only;
  //   - no console line or Sentry field holds an endpoint or a key;
  //   - the fence: only devices.ts reads or writes PushDevice rows; the schema's model and relation; the hand-written
  //     migration (additions only, safe to run twice); the generated client knows the model.
  //
  // Real: devices.ts, config.ts, user-service.ts (getUserById), reminder-cache.ts (its Redis is the fake). Faked at the
  // boundaries (scripts/lib/signin-world.ts): Prisma and Redis in memory, error capture. No network, no DB.
  //
  // Run with the fake env of scripts/ci-smokes.sh:
  //   $RUN smoke-push-devices

  import crypto from 'crypto';
  import fs from 'fs';
  import path from 'path';
  import { Prisma } from '@prisma/client';
  import {
    assert, baseUser, captured, check, DB, everyCapture, everyLog, fakePrisma, installSigninWorld, load, logs, prismaCalls,
    report, resetWorld, ROOT, show, type Row,
  } from './lib/signin-world';

  const MIGRATION = '20261106000000_push_devices';
  const read = (rel: string) => fs.readFileSync(path.join(ROOT, rel), 'utf8');
  const DAY = 86_400_000;

  const key65 = () => {
    const ecdh = crypto.createECDH('prime256v1');
    ecdh.generateKeys();
    return ecdh.getPublicKey().toString('base64url'); // 87 characters
  };
  const auth16 = () => crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('base64url'); // 22 characters
  const FCM = 'https://fcm.googleapis.com/fcm/send/dQw4w9WgXcQ:APA91bHPRgkF3JUikC4ENAHEeMrd41Zxv3hVZjC9KtT8OvPVGJ';
  const APPLE = 'https://web.push.apple.com/QGuQyavXutnMH7Yfc6PB9mxXzqrBm2rWvzGJ7oM8hGfTxwvW-5rNjzQZq0G9IBbx';
  const MOZILLA = 'https://updates.push.services.mozilla.com/wpush/v2/gAAAAABnYtEXAMPLEpushtoken_abc';
  const WNS = 'https://wns2-par02p.notify.windows.com/w/?token=BQYAAABexampleWnsToken%2bAbc%3d';
  const P256DH = key65();
  const AUTH = auth16();
  const input = (endpoint: string, p256dh = P256DH, auth = AUTH) => ({ endpoint, keys: { p256dh, auth } });

  /** Every endpoint and key of the run: none may reach a log line or a Sentry field. */
  const SECRETS: string[] = [FCM, APPLE, MOZILLA, WNS, 'dQw4w9WgXcQ', 'QGuQyavXutnMH7', 'gAAAAABnYtEXAMPLE', 'BQYAAABexample', P256DH, AUTH];

  /** The event alert cache re-syncs of a user: reminder-cache's own lines (an update, or a removal for one it doesn't admit). */
  const syncsOf = (id: number) => logs.filter(l => l === `[Reminder Cache] Updated user ${id} in cache` || l === `[Reminder Cache] Removed user ${id} from cache`).length;

  /** Every .ts/.tsx file under src/, app/ and components/, relative to the repo. */
  function codeFiles(): string[] {
    const out: string[] = [];
    const walk = (dir: string) => {
      for (const entry of fs.readdirSync(path.join(ROOT, dir), { withFileTypes: true })) {
        if (entry.name === 'node_modules' || entry.name.startsWith('.')) continue;
        const rel = `${dir}/${entry.name}`;
        if (entry.isDirectory()) walk(rel);
        else if (/\.tsx?$/.test(entry.name)) out.push(rel);
      }
    };
    for (const root of ['src', 'app', 'components']) walk(root);
    return out.sort();
  }

  function seed(): void {
    resetWorld();
    // 5: a web account with no chat; 6: another one; 8: a Telegram account with alerts on (the cache admits it)
    DB.user = [
      baseUser({ id: 5, name: 'Five', messagingPlatform: 'telegram' }),
      baseUser({ id: 6, name: 'Six' }),
      baseUser({ id: 8, name: 'Eight', telegramId: BigInt(5550008), remindersEnabled: true }),
    ];
    DB.pushDevice = [];
  }
  const rowsOf = (userId: number) => (DB.pushDevice ?? []).filter(r => r.userId === userId);

  async function main() {
    await installSigninWorld();
    const config = await load('src/services/web-push/config.ts').catch(() => ({} as Row));
    const devices = await load('src/services/web-push/devices.ts').catch(() => ({} as Row));

    await check('D1 config: push is on only with both VAPID keys set (blank or spaces is unset), the keys trimmed and read at call time; the contact claim is mailto:privacy@famcal.bot; PUSH_ENV is the two names', () => {
      const both = { VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY: ' pub-key ', VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY: 'priv-key' };
      assert(show(config.PUSH_ENV) === show(['VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY', 'VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY']), `PUSH_ENV ${show(config.PUSH_ENV)}`);
      const cases: [string, Row, string[]][] = [
        ['both', both, []],
        ['none', {}, ['VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY', 'VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY']],
        ['blank public', { ...both, VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY: '   ' }, ['VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY']],
        ['empty private', { ...both, VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY: '' }, ['VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY']],
      ];
      for (const [label, env, missing] of cases) {
        assert(show(config.missingPushEnv(env)) === show(missing), `${label}: missing ${show(config.missingPushEnv(env))}`);
        assert(config.pushConfigured(env) === (missing.length === 0), `${label}: pushConfigured`);
        assert(config.vapidPublicKey(env) === (missing.length === 0 ? 'pub-key' : null), `${label}: vapidPublicKey ${show(config.vapidPublicKey(env))}`);
        assert(show(config.vapidKeys(env)) === show(missing.length === 0 ? { publicKey: 'pub-key', privateKey: 'priv-key' } : null), `${label}: vapidKeys`);
      }
      // The default is process.env, read at the call
      const saved = [process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY, process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY];
      process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY = 'later-pub';
      process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY = 'later-priv';
      assert(config.vapidPublicKey() === 'later-pub', 'process.env is read at the call');
      delete process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY;
      assert(config.pushConfigured() === false, 'off once a key is gone');
      [process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY, process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY] = saved;
      if (saved[0] === undefined) delete process.env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY;
      if (saved[1] === undefined) delete process.env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY;
      assert(config.vapidSubject() === 'mailto:privacy@famcal.bot', `subject ${config.vapidSubject()}`);
      assert(!/process\.env\.NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID/.test(read('src/services/web-push/config.ts')), 'a NEXT_PUBLIC_ key');
    });

    await check('D2 isValidDeviceInput: an https endpoint of Google\'s, Apple\'s, Mozilla\'s or Microsoft\'s push service with base64url keys of the right lengths; any other host (a lookalike too), http, a port, user info, an endpoint over 1024 characters, a key too short, too long or not base64url, or any other shape is refused', () => {
      const good = [input(FCM), input(APPLE), input(MOZILLA), input(WNS), input(FCM, `${P256DH}=`, `${AUTH}==`)];
      for (const x of good) assert(devices.isValidDeviceInput(x) === true, `refused ${show(x)}`);
      const bad: [string, unknown][] = [
        ['null', null], ['a string', FCM], ['no keys', { endpoint: FCM }], ['keys not an object', { endpoint: FCM, keys: 'x' }],
        ['a number endpoint', { endpoint: 5, keys: { p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH } }],
        ['not a URL', input('fcm send')], ['http', input(FCM.replace('https:', 'http:'))],
        ['another host', input('https://example.test/fcm/send/abc')], ['a lookalike', input('https://fcm.googleapis.com.example.test/fcm/send/abc')],
        ['a name that ends like it', input('https://evilfcm.googleapis.com/fcm/send/abc')], ['a port', input('https://fcm.googleapis.com:8443/fcm/send/abc')],
        ['user info', input('https://user:pw@fcm.googleapis.com/fcm/send/abc')], ['over 1024', input(`${FCM}/${'a'.repeat(1000)}`)],
        ['p256dh short', input(FCM, 'abc')], ['p256dh long', input(FCM, `${P256DH}${'A'.repeat(20)}`)], ['p256dh not base64url', input(FCM, `${P256DH.slice(0, 80)}+/+/+/+`)],
        ['auth short', input(FCM, P256DH, 'abcdefghij')], ['auth long', input(FCM, P256DH, 'a'.repeat(40))], ['auth a number', { endpoint: FCM, keys: { p256dh: P256DH, auth: 7 } }],
      ];
      for (const [label, x] of bad) assert(devices.isValidDeviceInput(x) === false, `accepted ${label}`);
      assert(config.isPushServiceUrl(new URL(APPLE)) === true && config.isPushServiceUrl(new URL('https://push.apple.com.example.test/x')) === false, 'isPushServiceUrl');
      // isValidEndpoint is the endpoint half of the same rule (Remove a way reads it alone)
      for (const ok of [FCM, APPLE, MOZILLA, WNS]) assert(devices.isValidEndpoint(ok) === true, `isValidEndpoint refused ${ok.slice(0, 30)}`);
      for (const no of [5, null, 'fcm send', FCM.replace('https:', 'http:'), 'https://example.test/x', `${FCM}/${'a'.repeat(1000)}`]) assert(devices.isValidEndpoint(no) === false, `isValidEndpoint accepted ${show(no).slice(0, 30)}`);
    });

    await check('D3 addDevice: a new endpoint is one row (1 of 10); the same browser again keeps one row with its new keys and a new turn-on time; another account\'s endpoint moves (moved, both accounts re-synced); an 11th device removes the oldest and no other account\'s; one log line each, with ids and counts only', async () => {
      seed();
      const first = await devices.addDevice(5, input(FCM));
      assert(show(first) === show({ moved: false, count: 1 }) && rowsOf(5).length === 1, `first ${show(first)} ${show(rowsOf(5))}`);
      assert(rowsOf(5)[0].endpoint === FCM && rowsOf(5)[0].p256dh === P256DH && rowsOf(5)[0].auth === AUTH, 'the row holds the endpoint and keys');
      assert(syncsOf(5) === 1, `user 5 re-synced ${syncsOf(5)} times`);
      assert(logs.includes('[push-devices] user 5: a device turned on (1 of 10)'), `log ${show(logs)}`);
      // The same browser again: new keys, one row, a later turn-on time
      rowsOf(5)[0].createdAt = new Date(Date.now() - 3 * DAY);
      const newKey = key65();
      const again = await devices.addDevice(5, input(FCM, newKey));
      assert(show(again) === show({ moved: false, count: 1 }) && rowsOf(5).length === 1 && rowsOf(5)[0].p256dh === newKey, `again ${show(again)}`);
      assert(Date.now() - rowsOf(5)[0].createdAt.getTime() < 60_000, 'the turn-on time is now');
      // Another account turns the same browser on: the row moves
      logs.length = 0;
      const moved = await devices.addDevice(6, input(FCM));
      assert(show(moved) === show({ moved: true, count: 1 }) && rowsOf(5).length === 0 && rowsOf(6).length === 1, `moved ${show(moved)} ${show(DB.pushDevice)}`);
      assert(syncsOf(5) === 1 && syncsOf(6) === 1, `both re-synced: 5 ${syncsOf(5)}, 6 ${syncsOf(6)}`);
      assert(logs.includes('[push-devices] user 6: a device turned on (1 of 10), moved from user 5'), `log ${show(logs)}`);
      // Ten devices of user 5, then an 11th: the oldest goes, user 6's stays
      seed();
      DB.pushDevice = Array.from({ length: 10 }, (_, i) => ({ id: `d${i}`, userId: 5, endpoint: `${APPLE}${i}`, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date(Date.now() - (20 - i) * DAY) }));
      DB.pushDevice.push({ id: 'other', userId: 6, endpoint: MOZILLA, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date(Date.now() - 30 * DAY) });
      const eleventh = await devices.addDevice(5, input(FCM));
      assert(show(eleventh) === show({ moved: false, count: 10 }) && rowsOf(5).length === 10, `eleventh ${show(eleventh)}`);
      assert(!rowsOf(5).some(r => r.id === 'd0') && rowsOf(5).some(r => r.endpoint === FCM) && rowsOf(6).length === 1, `the oldest (d0) should go: ${show(rowsOf(5).map(r => r.id))}`);
      assert(logs.includes('[push-devices] user 5: a device turned on (10 of 10); the oldest 1 removed'), `log ${show(logs)}`);
    });

    await check('D4 isOwnDevice and deleteOwnDevice: only the caller\'s own row; another account\'s endpoint and an unknown one are "not mine" alike and stay; a delete re-syncs', async () => {
      seed();
      DB.pushDevice = [
        { id: 'a', userId: 5, endpoint: FCM, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
        { id: 'b', userId: 6, endpoint: APPLE, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
      ];
      assert(await devices.isOwnDevice(5, FCM) === true, 'own');
      assert(await devices.isOwnDevice(5, APPLE) === false && await devices.isOwnDevice(5, MOZILLA) === false, 'another\'s and unknown');
      assert(await devices.deleteOwnDevice(5, APPLE) === false && DB.pushDevice.length === 2 && syncsOf(6) === 0, 'another account\'s device is not deleted');
      assert(await devices.deleteOwnDevice(5, FCM) === true && rowsOf(5).length === 0 && syncsOf(5) === 1, 'own deleted and re-synced');
      assert(await devices.deleteOwnDevice(5, FCM) === false, 'twice: nothing left');
    });

    await check('D5 deleteDevicesOf: one account or several, all their rows; keep spares that endpoint; inside a caller\'s transaction it re-syncs nothing (the caller does, after the commit); outside it re-syncs each; no account asks nothing', async () => {
      seed();
      const put = () => {
        DB.pushDevice = [
          { id: 'a', userId: 5, endpoint: FCM, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
          { id: 'b', userId: 5, endpoint: APPLE, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
          { id: 'c', userId: 6, endpoint: MOZILLA, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
          { id: 'd', userId: 8, endpoint: WNS, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
        ];
      };
      put();
      assert(await devices.deleteDevicesOf(5, { keep: APPLE }) === 1 && show(DB.pushDevice.map((r: Row) => r.id)) === show(['b', 'c', 'd']), `keep ${show(DB.pushDevice)}`);
      assert(syncsOf(5) === 1, 'outside a transaction: re-synced');
      put();
      logs.length = 0;
      const inTx = await fakePrisma.$transaction(async (tx: Row) => devices.deleteDevicesOf([5, 6], { tx }));
      assert(inTx === 3 && show(DB.pushDevice.map((r: Row) => r.id)) === show(['d']), `in a transaction ${inTx} ${show(DB.pushDevice)}`);
      assert(syncsOf(5) === 0 && syncsOf(6) === 0, 'inside a transaction: the caller re-syncs');
      put();
      assert(await devices.deleteDevicesOf([5, 6, 5], { keep: null }) === 3 && syncsOf(5) === 1 && syncsOf(6) === 1, 'several, each re-synced once');
      prismaCalls.length = 0;
      assert(await devices.deleteDevicesOf([]) === 0 && !prismaCalls.some(c => c.startsWith('pushDevice.')), `no account: ${show(prismaCalls)}`);
    });

    await check('D6 devicesForSend gives the four fields of the account\'s rows, oldest first; deleteGoneDevice deletes the row and re-syncs its account; a row already gone is a log line, never a throw', async () => {
      seed();
      DB.pushDevice = [
        { id: 'new', userId: 5, endpoint: APPLE, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
        { id: 'old', userId: 5, endpoint: FCM, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date(Date.now() - DAY) },
        { id: 'his', userId: 6, endpoint: MOZILLA, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() },
      ];
      const got = await devices.devicesForSend(5);
      assert(show(got) === show([{ id: 'old', endpoint: FCM, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH }, { id: 'new', endpoint: APPLE, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH }]), `devicesForSend ${show(got)}`);
      await devices.deleteGoneDevice('old');
      assert(rowsOf(5).length === 1 && syncsOf(5) === 1, `gone ${show(rowsOf(5))}`);
      await devices.deleteGoneDevice('old');
      assert(logs.includes('[push-devices] device old was already gone') && captured.length === 0, `already gone: ${show(logs)} ${show(captured)}`);
    });

    await check('D7 afterDevicesChanged never throws: a failed account read is logged and reported by its kind only (no endpoint, even when the error\'s message holds one), and a change still answers', async () => {
      seed();
      DB.pushDevice = [{ id: 'a', userId: 5, endpoint: FCM, p256dh: P256DH, auth: AUTH, createdAt: new Date() }];
      const users = fakePrisma.user as Row;
      const real = users.findUnique;
      users.findUnique = async () => { throw Object.assign(new Error(`Invalid \`prisma.user.findUnique()\` invocation near ${FCM}`), { name: 'PrismaClientValidationError' }); };
      try {
        assert(await devices.deleteOwnDevice(5, FCM) === true, 'the delete still answers');
      } finally {
        users.findUnique = real;
      }
      assert(logs.some(l => l.startsWith('[push-devices] user 5: the alert cache entry was not synced: PrismaClientValidationError')), `log ${show(logs)}`);
      assert(captured.some(c => c.context === 'push-devices' && c.extra.user_id === 5), `captured ${show(captured)}`);
      await devices.afterDevicesChanged([404]);
      assert(logs.includes('[push-devices] user 404: no account, no alert cache entry to sync'), `a missing account: ${show(logs)}`);
    });

    await check('D8 no console line or Sentry field of the run holds an endpoint, a push service token or a key', () => {
      const text = show(everyLog) + show(everyCapture);
      const found = SECRETS.filter(s => text.includes(s));
      assert(found.length === 0, `leaked: ${found.map(s => s.slice(0, 24)).join(', ')}`);
    });

    await check('D9 the fence and the data: only src/services/web-push/devices.ts reads or writes PushDevice rows (no other .pushDevice and no raw "PushDevice"); the schema has the model and User.pushDevices; the migration makes the table, its unique endpoint, its user index and its cascading key, only adds, and is safe to run twice; the generated client knows the model', () => {
      const ALLOWED = 'src/services/web-push/devices.ts';
      const touching = codeFiles().filter(rel => /\.pushDevice\b/.test(read(rel)) || /"PushDevice"/.test(read(rel)));
      assert(touching.includes(ALLOWED), `devices.ts touches no PushDevice row (found ${show(touching)})`);
      const stray = touching.filter(rel => rel !== ALLOWED);
      assert(stray.length === 0, `PushDevice rows touched outside devices.ts: ${show(stray)}`);
      const schema = read('prisma/schema.prisma');
      const model = /model PushDevice \{([\s\S]*?)\n\}/.exec(schema)?.[1] ?? '';
      const lines = model.split('\n').map(l => l.replace(/\/\/.*$/, '').trim().replace(/\s+/g, ' ')).filter(Boolean);
      for (const want of [
        'id String @id @default(cuid())', 'userId Int', 'user User @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)',
        'endpoint String @unique', 'p256dh String', 'auth String', 'createdAt DateTime @default(now())', '@@index([userId])',
      ]) assert(lines.includes(want), `PushDevice lacks "${want}": ${show(lines)}`);
      const user = /model User \{([\s\S]*?)\n\}/.exec(schema)?.[1] ?? '';
      assert(/^\s*pushDevices\s+PushDevice\[\]\s*$/m.test(user), 'User has no pushDevices PushDevice[]');
      const file = path.join(ROOT, 'prisma/migrations', MIGRATION, 'migration.sql');
      assert(fs.existsSync(file), `no prisma/migrations/${MIGRATION}/migration.sql`);
      const sql = fs.readFileSync(file, 'utf8').split('\n').filter(l => !l.trim().startsWith('--')).join(' ').replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim();
      for (const want of [
        'CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS "PushDevice" ( "id" TEXT NOT NULL, "userId" INTEGER NOT NULL, "endpoint" TEXT NOT NULL, "p256dh" TEXT NOT NULL, "auth" TEXT NOT NULL, "createdAt" TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, CONSTRAINT "PushDevice_pkey" PRIMARY KEY ("id") );',
        'CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "PushDevice_endpoint_key" ON "PushDevice"("endpoint");',
        'CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "PushDevice_userId_idx" ON "PushDevice"("userId");',
        'DO $$ BEGIN ALTER TABLE "PushDevice" ADD CONSTRAINT "PushDevice_userId_fkey" FOREIGN KEY ("userId") REFERENCES "User"("id") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE; EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object THEN null; END $$;',
      ]) assert(sql.includes(want), `the migration lacks: ${want}`);
      assert(!/\b(DROP|DELETE|UPDATE|RENAME|TRUNCATE)\b/i.test(sql.replace(/ON (DELETE|UPDATE) CASCADE/g, '')), `the migration does more than add: ${sql}`);
      const fields = Object.values((Prisma as unknown as Record<string, Record<string, string> | undefined>).PushDeviceScalarFieldEnum ?? {});
      assert(show(fields) === show(['id', 'userId', 'endpoint', 'p256dh', 'auth', 'createdAt']), `the generated client's PushDevice: ${show(fields)} (run npx prisma generate)`);
    });

    report();
  }

  main().catch(err => {
    console.error(err);
    process.exit(1);
  });
  ```

      const both = { VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY: 'VALUE-VPUB-1', VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY: 'VALUE-VPRIV-2' };
      const cases: [string, Record<string, string | undefined>, string[]][] = [
        ['both set', both, []],
        ['none set', {}, ['Browser notifications are off: VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY and VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY are not set']],
        ['only the private key missing', { ...both, VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY: undefined }, ['Browser notifications are off: VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY is not set']],
        ['the public key blank', { ...both, VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY: '  ' }, ['Browser notifications are off: VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY is not set']],
      ];
      for (const [label, env, want] of cases) {
        const got = typeof mod.pushSetupWarnings === 'function' ? mod.pushSetupWarnings(env) : 'no pushSetupWarnings';
        if (show(got) !== show(want)) problems.push(`${label}: ${show(got)}`);
        if (/VALUE-/.test(show(got))) problems.push(`${label}: a value in ${show(got)}`);
        if (config.pushConfigured?.(env) !== (want.length === 0)) problems.push(`${label}: pushConfigured disagrees with the warning`);
      }
      const pattern = /const SECRET_NAME = \/(.+)\/i;/.exec(read('src/lib/console-scrub.ts'))?.[1];
      assert(pattern, 'no SECRET_NAME in src/lib/console-scrub.ts');
      if (!new RegExp(pattern, 'i').test('VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY')) problems.push('VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY does not match the console scrubber\'s SECRET_NAME');
      const boot = read('instrumentation.ts');
      const production = boot.indexOf("if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production') {");
      const line = boot.indexOf('for (const warning of pushSetupWarnings(process.env)) console.warn(`[startup] ${warning}`);');
      if (production < 0 || line < production) problems.push('instrumentation.ts does not log pushSetupWarnings in its production block');
      if (!/import\('\.\/src\/lib\/startup-checks'\)/.test(boot) || !/\bpushSetupWarnings\b.*=\s*await import\('\.\/src\/lib\/startup-checks'\)/.test(boot)) problems.push('instrumentation.ts does not import pushSetupWarnings from startup-checks');
      const readme = read('README.md');
      const example = read('.env.example').split('\n');
      for (const name of PUSH_NAMES) {
        if (!new RegExp(`^\\|[^\\n]*\`${name}\``, 'm').test(readme)) problems.push(`README's table has no ${name} row`);
        const placeholder = example.find((l) => l.startsWith(`# ${name}=`));
        if (!placeholder || !/^# [A-Z_]+=your_[a-z_]+_here$/.test(placeholder)) problems.push(`.env.example has no commented placeholder for ${name}`);
        if (example.some((l) => l.startsWith(`${name}=`))) problems.push(`.env.example sets ${name} uncommented`);
      }
      assert(problems.length === 0, problems.join('; '));
    });

  ```prisma

  // A browser that turned FamCal's notifications on (spec 2026-11-05-web-push §3.1): the address its push service gave it
  // (endpoint) and the two keys that encrypt a notification so only that browser can read it. One row per browser, on one
  // account at a time, at most 10 per account; read and written only in src/services/web-push/devices.ts. Deleted with
  // the account, and by the writers that end its sessions (sessionsValidAfter) or give it a chat.
  model PushDevice {
    id        String   @id @default(cuid())
    userId    Int
    user      User     @relation(fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)
    endpoint  String   @unique
    p256dh    String
    auth      String
    createdAt DateTime @default(now())

    @@index([userId])
  }
  ```

  Then `npx prisma generate` (no database needed). Optional cross-check, also no database: `npx prisma migrate diff --from-empty --to-schema-datamodel prisma/schema.prisma --script | sed -n '/"PushDevice"/,/;/p'` shows Prisma's own SQL for the table; the columns, index names and key in 3c must match it.

  **3c. `prisma/migrations/20261106000000_push_devices/migration.sql`** (new):

  ```sql
  -- Notifications in the browser (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-web-push-design.md §3.1).
  --
  -- "PushDevice": a browser that turned FamCal's notifications on. "endpoint" is the address its push service gave that
  -- browser, unique, so a browser is on one account at a time; "p256dh" and "auth" are the two keys that encrypt a
  -- notification for it. At most 10 per account (src/services/web-push/devices.ts, the only file that reads or writes
  -- them); deleted with the account (ON DELETE CASCADE).
  --
  -- Additions only, so one push is enough: the previous build, still serving while this runs, never reads or writes
  -- it. Hand-written and idempotent, like the other recent migrations: each is made only when missing, so a re-run is
  -- a no-op.

  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS "PushDevice" (
      "id" TEXT NOT NULL,
      "userId" INTEGER NOT NULL,
      "endpoint" TEXT NOT NULL,
      "p256dh" TEXT NOT NULL,
      "auth" TEXT NOT NULL,
      "createdAt" TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,

      CONSTRAINT "PushDevice_pkey" PRIMARY KEY ("id")
  );

  CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "PushDevice_endpoint_key" ON "PushDevice"("endpoint");
  CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS "PushDevice_userId_idx" ON "PushDevice"("userId");

  DO $$ BEGIN
    ALTER TABLE "PushDevice" ADD CONSTRAINT "PushDevice_userId_fkey"
      FOREIGN KEY ("userId") REFERENCES "User"("id") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;
  EXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object THEN null;
  END $$;
  ```

  Never run `npm run build` or `prisma migrate deploy` (they reach prod); the migration applies on deploy.

  **3d. `src/services/web-push/config.ts`** (new):

  ```ts
  /**
   * Browser notifications' setup (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-web-push-design.md §3.9): the VAPID key pair,
   * read from the environment at each call (never NEXT_PUBLIC_, never at build), FamCal's contact claim, and the push
   * services a browser's subscription may point at. Push stays off until both keys are set: the props' key is null, the
   * routes answer 503, the senders skip, and production startup warns (startup-checks.ts pushSetupWarnings).
   * The private key leaves this file only through vapidKeys (send.ts signs with it). Server only.
   */

  import { SELLER } from '../../lib/seller';

  export const PUSH_ENV = ['VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY', 'VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY'] as const;

  /** The VAPID variables that are unset or blank, in PUSH_ENV's order: the one rule pushConfigured and the startup warning read. */
  export function missingPushEnv(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string[] {
    return PUSH_ENV.filter((name) => !env[name]?.trim());
  }

  export function pushConfigured(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): boolean {
    return missingPushEnv(env).length === 0;
  }

  /** The public key the browser subscribes with, or null while push is off. */
  export function vapidPublicKey(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): string | null {
    return pushConfigured(env) ? env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY!.trim() : null;
  }

  /** Both keys, for signing (send.ts), or null while push is off. */
  export function vapidKeys(env: NodeJS.ProcessEnv = process.env): { publicKey: string; privateKey: string } | null {
    if (!pushConfigured(env)) return null;
    return { publicKey: env.VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY!.trim(), privateKey: env.VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY!.trim() };
  }

  /** The contact the push services see in every VAPID token: FamCal's own address (Apple refuses a localhost one). */
  export function vapidSubject(): string {
    return `mailto:${SELLER.email}`;
  }

  /**
   * The push services a device's endpoint may point at, by host (the host itself, or one under it): Google's FCM (Chrome,
   * Edge on Android, Brave, Opera, Samsung Internet), Apple's (Safari, iPhone and iPad), Mozilla's (Firefox) and
   * Microsoft's WNS (Edge on Windows). The server POSTs to every stored endpoint, so one anywhere else is refused
   * (devices.ts isValidDeviceInput). src/lib/sentry-scrub.ts scrubs the paths of these hosts; scripts/smoke-sentry-scrub.ts
   * holds the two lists together.
   */
  export const PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS = ['fcm.googleapis.com', 'push.apple.com', 'push.services.mozilla.com', 'notify.windows.com'] as const;

  /** An https URL of a known push service, on the default port, with no user name or password. */
  export function isPushServiceUrl(url: URL): boolean {
    if (url.protocol !== 'https:' || url.port !== '' || url.username !== '' || url.password !== '') return false;
    const host = url.hostname.toLowerCase();
    return PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS.some((service) => host === service || host.endsWith(`.${service}`));
  }
  ```

  **3e. `src/services/web-push/devices.ts`** (new):

  ```ts
  /**
   * The browsers that get an account's notifications (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-web-push-design.md §3.1,
   * §2.4): one PushDevice row per browser, on one account at a time, at most MAX_DEVICES per account. The only file that
   * reads or writes the table (scripts/smoke-push-devices.ts D9 fences it, as smoke-signin-setup fences SignInIdentity).
   *
   * An endpoint and its keys are a credential: no console line, Sentry field, cache entry or admin view holds one. Lines
   * name the user id, the device id and counts. A failure is told by its kind only (safe-error.ts): a Prisma error's
   * message can print the query's values. The functions a route calls (addDevice, isOwnDevice, deleteOwnDevice,
   * deleteDevicesOf) let a database error through to it; deleteGoneDevice and afterDevicesChanged never throw.
   *
   * Every change of an account's devices re-syncs its event alert cache entry (afterDevicesChanged), since a device is
   * what lets an account with no chat get alerts. A change made inside a caller's transaction is re-synced by the caller,
   * after the commit.
   */

  import type { Prisma } from '@prisma/client';
  import { prisma } from '../../utils/prisma';
  import { captureError } from '../../lib/error-capture';
  import { SafeSignInError, safeErrorSummary } from '../web-signin/safe-error';
  import { isPushServiceUrl } from './config';

  export const MAX_DEVICES = 10;

  export interface PushKeys {
    p256dh: string;
    auth: string;
  }

  /** What a browser's PushSubscription.toJSON() carries that we keep. */
  export interface PushDeviceInput {
    endpoint: string;
    keys: PushKeys;
  }

  export interface StoredDevice {
    id: string;
    endpoint: string;
    p256dh: string;
    auth: string;
  }

  const MAX_ENDPOINT_LENGTH = 1024;
  /** base64url, with the padding a browser may add */
  const BASE64URL = /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+={0,2}$/;

  const keyOk = (value: unknown, min: number, max: number): boolean =>
    typeof value === 'string' && value.length >= min && value.length <= max && BASE64URL.test(value);

  /**
   * An endpoint we would store or keep: an https URL of a known push service (config.ts PUSH_SERVICE_HOSTS) of at most
   * 1024 characters. The one endpoint rule: isValidDeviceInput reads it, and so does Remove a way (Task 6).
   */
  export function isValidEndpoint(x: unknown): x is string {
    if (typeof x !== 'string' || x.length > MAX_ENDPOINT_LENGTH) return false;
    try {
      return isPushServiceUrl(new URL(x));
    } catch {
      return false; // not a URL: refused, and the route logs the refusal
    }
  }

  /**
   * A subscription we can store: a valid endpoint (isValidEndpoint), a p256dh of 80-100 characters (65 bytes) and an
   * auth of 16-32 (16 bytes), both base64url.
   */
  export function isValidDeviceInput(x: unknown): x is PushDeviceInput {
    if (!x || typeof x !== 'object') return false;
    const { endpoint, keys } = x as { endpoint?: unknown; keys?: unknown };
    if (!isValidEndpoint(endpoint)) return false;
    if (!keys || typeof keys !== 'object') return false;
    const { p256dh, auth } = keys as { p256dh?: unknown; auth?: unknown };
    return keyOk(p256dh, 80, 100) && keyOk(auth, 16, 32);
  }

  /**
   * Stores this browser for the account: a new row, or the row of the same endpoint with its new keys, its new account
   * (it moves from another one: the browser proved it holds it) and a new turn-on time. Then the account's oldest
   * devices beyond MAX_DEVICES are removed. Re-syncs every account touched after the commit.
   */
  export async function addDevice(userId: number, input: PushDeviceInput): Promise<{ moved: boolean; count: number }> {
    const { endpoint, keys: { p256dh, auth } } = input;
    const { from, count, dropped } = await prisma.$transaction(async (tx) => {
      const before = await tx.pushDevice.findUnique({ where: { endpoint }, select: { userId: true } });
      await tx.pushDevice.upsert({
        where: { endpoint },
        create: { userId, endpoint, p256dh, auth },
        update: { userId, p256dh, auth, createdAt: new Date() },
      });
      const mine = await tx.pushDevice.findMany({ where: { userId }, orderBy: [{ createdAt: 'desc' }, { id: 'desc' }], select: { id: true } });
      const extra = mine.slice(MAX_DEVICES).map((device) => device.id);
      if (extra.length > 0) await tx.pushDevice.deleteMany({ where: { id: { in: extra } } });
      return { from: before?.userId ?? null, count: mine.length - extra.length, dropped: extra.length };
    });
    const others = from !== null && from !== userId ? [from] : [];
    console.log(
      `[push-devices] user ${userId}: a device turned on (${count} of ${MAX_DEVICES})` +
        (others.length > 0 ? `, moved from user ${others[0]}` : '') +
        (dropped > 0 ? `; the oldest ${dropped} removed` : ''),
    );
    await afterDevicesChanged([userId, ...others]);
    return { moved: others.length > 0, count };
  }

  /** Whether this endpoint is one of the account's devices. Another account's and an unknown one read the same. */
  export async function isOwnDevice(userId: number, endpoint: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const row = await prisma.pushDevice.findUnique({ where: { endpoint }, select: { userId: true } });
    return row?.userId === userId;
  }

  /** Deletes this endpoint only when it is the account's own; true when a row went. */
  export async function deleteOwnDevice(userId: number, endpoint: string): Promise<boolean> {
    const { count } = await prisma.pushDevice.deleteMany({ where: { userId, endpoint } });
    if (count === 0) return false;
    console.log(`[push-devices] user ${userId}: a device turned off`);
    await afterDevicesChanged([userId]);
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * Deletes every device of these accounts, except the endpoint `keep` (the browser a Remove was pressed in). Inside the
   * caller's transaction (`tx`) it re-syncs nothing: the caller calls afterDevicesChanged after the commit. Returns how
   * many went.
   */
  export async function deleteDevicesOf(
    userIds: number | number[],
    opts: { keep?: string | null; tx?: Prisma.TransactionClient } = {},
  ): Promise<number> {
    const ids = [...new Set(([] as number[]).concat(userIds))];
    if (ids.length === 0) return 0;
    const client = opts.tx ?? prisma;
    const { count } = await client.pushDevice.deleteMany({
      where: { userId: { in: ids }, ...(opts.keep ? { endpoint: { not: opts.keep } } : {}) },
    });
    if (count > 0) console.log(`[push-devices] users ${ids.join(', ')}: ${count} devices deleted${opts.keep ? ' (the device in use kept)' : ''}`);
    if (!opts.tx && count > 0) await afterDevicesChanged(ids);
    return count;
  }

  /** The account's devices, oldest first, for one send (send.ts reads them at send time). */
  export async function devicesForSend(userId: number): Promise<StoredDevice[]> {
    return prisma.pushDevice.findMany({
      where: { userId },
      orderBy: { createdAt: 'asc' },
      select: { id: true, endpoint: true, p256dh: true, auth: true },
    });
  }

  /** A device its push service called gone (404 or 410): deleted, and its account re-synced. Never throws. */
  export async function deleteGoneDevice(id: string): Promise<void> {
    let userId: number;
    try {
      ({ userId } = await prisma.pushDevice.delete({ where: { id }, select: { userId: true } }));
    } catch (error) {
      if ((error as { code?: unknown } | null)?.code === 'P2025') {
        console.log(`[push-devices] device ${id} was already gone`);
        return;
      }
      console.error(`[push-devices] device ${id}: could not delete it: ${safeErrorSummary(error)}`);
      captureError(new SafeSignInError(`push-devices: ${safeErrorSummary(error)}`), 'push-devices', { purpose: 'delete-gone' });
      return;
    }
    console.log(`[push-devices] user ${userId}: device ${id} deleted (its push service says it is gone)`);
    await afterDevicesChanged([userId]);
  }

  /**
   * Puts each account's event alert cache entry in line with its row (reminder-cache.ts syncReminderCacheEntry, the
   * hourly sync's rule). Never throws: a failure is logged and reported, and the hourly sync mends the entry.
   */
  export async function afterDevicesChanged(userIds: number[]): Promise<void> {
    for (const userId of [...new Set(userIds)]) {
      try {
        // Loaded here: user-service imports user-moderation, which deletes devices through this file
        const { getUserById } = await import('../user-service');
        const { syncReminderCacheEntry } = await import('../reminder-cache');
        const user = await getUserById(userId);
        if (!user) {
          console.log(`[push-devices] user ${userId}: no account, no alert cache entry to sync`);
          continue;
        }
        await syncReminderCacheEntry(user);
      } catch (error) {
        console.error(`[push-devices] user ${userId}: the alert cache entry was not synced: ${safeErrorSummary(error)}`);
        captureError(new SafeSignInError(`push-devices: ${safeErrorSummary(error)}`), 'push-devices', { user_id: userId, purpose: 'cache-sync' });
      }
    }
  }
  ```

  `isValidEndpoint`'s `catch` holds a comment and a `return`, not an empty body (no silent failure: the route logs the 400).

  // src/services/zmanim/pause.ts
  export async function nextPauseStart(user: PauseUser, from: Date, withinMs: number): Promise<Date | null>;
  // src/services/web-push/payload.ts

  export function buildPushPayload(m: PushMessage): string;

  // src/services/web-push/send.ts
  export type PushKind = 'reminder' | 'alert' | 'briefing' | 'partner-accepted' | 'partner-done' | 'reconnect' | 'test';
  export const PUSH_LIFETIME_S: Record<PushKind, number>;
  export const PUSH_URGENCY: Record<PushKind, 'high' | 'normal' | 'low'>;
  export const PAUSED_KINDS: readonly PushKind[];
  export type PushUser = PauseUser;
  export interface PushSendInput extends PushMessage { kind: PushKind; until?: Date; onlyEndpoint?: string }
  export interface PushSendResult { sent: number; total: number; retriable: boolean }
  export async function pushTtlSeconds(user: PushUser, kind: PushKind, now: Date, until?: Date): Promise<number>;

- Produces (beyond the contract):
  ```ts
  // src/services/zmanim/pause-windows.ts

  // src/services/web-push/send.ts
  export const MAX_PUSH_TTL_S = 86_400;
  export function __resetPushForTests(): void; // smokes only: forgets the VAPID headers and the once-a-day reports
  ```
- Meaning of `retriable` (for Tasks 8-11): true when no device took the push and at least one answered 429, 5xx or nothing (the loop gave up), and also when the device read itself failed (a database blip). A reminder's claim is let go then; false otherwise (gone, refused, no devices, push off).
- `url` in `PushMessage` is an absolute `https://famcal.bot/...` URL (Apple's declarative `navigate`); callers build it.

  // Smoke test for the browser notification sender (spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-web-push-design.md §3.3, §3.4,
  // §3.6; plan 6c-2 Task 2): src/services/web-push/payload.ts and send.ts.
  //
  // What it pins:
  //   - the payload: Apple's declarative JSON for every browser (web_push 8030, title, body, navigate, lang, dir, tag,
  //     silent), at most 2048 bytes of UTF-8, a long title then the body cut with "…" (never half a character); the Topic,
  //     32 base64url characters of a SHA-256 of the tag, carrying no tag text;
  //   - the lifetime (TTL): the kind's own, an alert's event start, and for a scheduled kind the next Shabbat or Yom Tov
  //     window (asked of nextPauseStart, and only for those kinds), never above a day, never below a second;
  //   - the request: web-push builds and encrypts it (the body decrypts, with the subscriber's keys, to the payload),
  //     fetchWithRetry sends it, with TTL, Urgency, Topic and aes128gcm; the VAPID header is signed by our key with
  //     FamCal's contact, one per push service, kept for an hour and made anew after it;
  //   - the answers: any 2xx is sent; 404 and 410 delete the device; 429, 5xx and no answer are retried by the loop and
  //     a give-up is retriable; a Retry-After over 10 s fails at once; 400 and 413 are reported; 401 and 403 are reported
  //     once a day per device;
  //   - push off (no keys) and no devices send nothing; a device whose stored key the library refuses is skipped and
  //     reported while the others go; a failed device read is retriable; it never throws; one log line per send;
  //   - no console line or Sentry field holds an endpoint, a key, the title or the body;

  // pause's next window (pause.ts; scripts/smoke-pause.ts tests the real one), the push services (fetch), error capture

  import crypto from 'crypto';
  import fs from 'fs';
  import path from 'path';

    assert, captured, check, everyCapture, everyLog, installSigninWorld, load, logs, report, resetWorld, ROOT, show, stubModule, type Row,
